<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0581: Unsoundness in heapless</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0581.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="memory-corruption"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0581.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0581: Unsoundness in heapless"/>
<published>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">`Deque::make_contiguous` could lead to broken invariants</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/heapless/RUSTSEC-2026-0581.md">History</a> ⋅

</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0581
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>
<code>Deque::make_contiguous</code> could lead to broken invariants</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-19">
November 19, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/heapless.html">heapless</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/heapless">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
<span class="tag info">INFO</span>
Unsound
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/memory-corruption.html">memory-corruption</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/out-of-bounds.html">#out-of-bounds</a>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/issues/694">
https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/issues/694
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/issues/676">
https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/issues/676
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::deque::Deque::make_contiguous</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>In some branches, <code>Deque::make_contiguous</code> set the <code>back</code> value to either <code>0</code> or <code>N</code> when it shouldn't, leading to broken invariants and out-of-bounds writes.</p>
<p>Reported by <a href="https://github.com/DRMacIver">DRMacIver</a> and <a href="https://github.com/MJUCOM">https://github.com/MJUCOM</a>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0575: Unsoundness in heapless</title>

<summary xml:lang="en">`Spsc::peek` used `Relaxed` ordering where it should have use `Acquire`</summary>

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0575
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>
<code>Spsc::peek</code> used <code>Relaxed</code> ordering where it should have use <code>Acquire</code>
</p>
</span>
</header>

<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026

</dd>

<dd>
<span class="tag info">INFO</span>
Unsound
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>

</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>

</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>

</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>This could lead to UB through a race condition.</p>
<p>Reported by <a href="https://github.com/Eh2406">Jacob Finkelman</a>.</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0582: Unsoundness in heapless</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0582.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="memory-corruption"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0582.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0582: Unsoundness in heapless"/>
<published>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">On maps of `u16::MAX` elements, `IndexMap::insert` could lead to a broken invariant</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/heapless/RUSTSEC-2026-0582.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/heapless/RUSTSEC-2026-0582.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0582">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0582
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>On maps of <code>u16::MAX</code> elements, <code>IndexMap::insert</code> could lead to a broken invariant</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-19">
November 19, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/heapless.html">heapless</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/heapless">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
<span class="tag info">INFO</span>
Unsound
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/memory-corruption.html">memory-corruption</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/issues/672">
https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/issues/672
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::index_map::IndexMap::insert</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Note that this required filling up the map and having the last item inserted to hash to <code>u16::MAX</code>. It is therefore very unlikely to be reachable in a real-world scenario.</p>
<p>Reported by <a href="https://github.com/8rsrf7">8rsrf7</a>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0578: Unsoundness in heapless</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0578.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="memory-corruption"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0578.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0578: Unsoundness in heapless"/>
<published>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">`SortedLinkedList::zeroize` lead to an inconsistent state</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/heapless/RUSTSEC-2026-0578.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/heapless/RUSTSEC-2026-0578.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0578">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0578
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>
<code>SortedLinkedList::zeroize</code> lead to an inconsistent state</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-19">
November 19, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/heapless.html">heapless</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/heapless">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
<span class="tag info">INFO</span>
Unsound
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/memory-corruption.html">memory-corruption</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/pull/709">
https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/pull/709
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::sorted_linked_list::SortedLinkedList::zeroize</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>The <code>Zeroize</code> implementation of <code>SortedLinkedList</code> did not properly re-initialize the linked list, leading to broken invariants and memorry corruption.</p>
<p>Reported by <a href="https://github.com/Eh2406">Jacob Finkelman</a>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0576: Unsoundness in heapless</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0576.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="memory-corruption"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0576.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0576: Unsoundness in heapless"/>
<published>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">`IndexMap::truncate` could lead to an inconsistent state</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0576
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>
<code>IndexMap::truncate</code> could lead to an inconsistent state</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-19">
November 19, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/heapless.html">heapless</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/heapless">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
<span class="tag info">INFO</span>
Unsound
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/memory-corruption.html">memory-corruption</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/issues/674">
https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/issues/674
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::index_map::IndexMap::truncate</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>
<code>IndexMap::truncate</code> was not rebuilding indices when it should, leading to an inconsistent state and memory corruption.</p>
<p>Reported by <a href="https://github.com/Eh2406">Jacob Finkelman</a>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0579: Unsoundness in heapless</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0579.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="memory-corruption"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0579.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0579: Unsoundness in heapless"/>
<published>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">The `Deque` implementation from `TryFrom` was wrong when the `Deque` ended up full</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/heapless/RUSTSEC-2026-0579.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/heapless/RUSTSEC-2026-0579.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0579">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0579
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>The <code>Deque</code> implementation from <code>TryFrom</code> was wrong when the <code>Deque</code> ended up full</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-19">
November 19, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/heapless.html">heapless</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/heapless">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
<span class="tag info">INFO</span>
Unsound
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/memory-corruption.html">memory-corruption</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/out-of-bounds.html">#out-of-bounds</a>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/pull/702">
https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/pull/702
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::Deque::try_from</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.2, &#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>If the array passed to <code>Deque::try_from</code> was as long as the capacity of the <code>Deque</code>, it would lead to a invalid state.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0580: Unsoundness in heapless</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0580.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="memory-corruption"/>
<category term="thread-safety"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0580.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0580: Unsoundness in heapless"/>
<published>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">The treiber stack implementation in heapless had a race condition</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/heapless/RUSTSEC-2026-0580.md">History</a> ⋅

</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0580
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>The treiber stack implementation in heapless had a race condition</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-19">
November 19, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/heapless.html">heapless</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/heapless">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
<span class="tag info">INFO</span>
Unsound
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/memory-corruption.html">memory-corruption</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="/categories/thread-safety.html">thread-safety</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/race-condition.html">#race-condition</a>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/pull/704">
https://github.com/rust-embedded/heapless/pull/704
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::arc_pool</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::box_pool</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::object_pool</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.9.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt>
<code>heapless::pool</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.8.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>In the affected versions, it was possible for two threads or execution contexts running concurrently to race, causing one to read out of a pointer that had been invalidated by another execution context.</p>
<p>Reported by <a href="https://github.com/Eh2406">Jacob Finkelman</a>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0572: Vulnerability in wasmtime</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0572.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0572.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0572: Vulnerability in wasmtime"/>
<published>2026-11-19T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Panic when a component cancels a stream or future copy on the wrong end</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/wasmtime/RUSTSEC-2026-0572.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0572
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Panic when a component cancels a stream or future copy on the wrong end</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-16">
November 16, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-19">
November 19, 2026
</time>

<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0564: Vulnerability in lettre</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0564.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-18T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0564.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0564: Vulnerability in lettre"/>
<published>2026-11-18T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Panic on a mailbox display name containing CR or LF allows denial of service</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/lettre/RUSTSEC-2026-0564.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/lettre/RUSTSEC-2026-0564.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0564">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0564
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Panic on a mailbox display name containing CR or LF allows denial of service</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/lettre.html">lettre</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/lettre">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/email.html">#email</a>
<a href="/keywords/header.html">#header</a>
<a href="/keywords/panic.html">#panic</a>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/0ad883cdb20c256ab29c9d002fd4cc55c5d8abfe">
https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/0ad883cdb20c256ab29c9d002fd4cc55c5d8abfe
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.11.24</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.10.0-rc.5</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A <code>Mailbox</code> whose display name contains a CR or LF panics when it is formatted
or put in a header such as <code>From</code> or <code>To</code>. Someone who can choose a display
name, for example the name of a user a message is sent to, can crash the
thread or task building the message.</p>
<p>Applications that take display names only from trusted input are not exposed.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A malicious or compromised server can reply to <code>EHLO</code> with a line holding
nothing but white space, causing the client to panic.</p>
<p>Applications that connect only to a trusted server over implicit TLS are not
exposed; the risk applies to clients that may connect to untrusted servers, or
that use <code>STARTTLS</code> or no TLS, where a man-in-the-middle can forge the reply.</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0561: Vulnerability in lettre</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0561.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-18T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="format-injection"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0561.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0561: Vulnerability in lettre"/>
<published>2026-11-18T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Missing dot-stuffing after a bare LF or CR allows SMTP command injection</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/lettre/RUSTSEC-2026-0561.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/lettre/RUSTSEC-2026-0561.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0561">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0561
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Missing dot-stuffing after a bare LF or CR allows SMTP command injection</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/lettre.html">lettre</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/lettre">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/format-injection.html">format-injection</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/smtp.html">#smtp</a>
<a href="/keywords/smuggling.html">#smuggling</a>
<a href="/keywords/injection.html">#injection</a>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/3d9a6e6a467c6ce00bd39ada6aa1a00e57540a1d">
https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/3d9a6e6a467c6ce00bd39ada6aa1a00e57540a1d
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="related">Related</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2021-0069">RUSTSEC-2021-0069</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.11.24</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Someone who controls the content of a message with bare LF or CR line endings
can end it early with <code>\n.\n</code> on a server that takes a bare LF for a line
ending. What follows is read by the server as SMTP commands, which can be used
to send other messages over the same authenticated connection.</p>
<p>This affects messages passed to <code>send_raw</code>, and message bodies that are sent
without being encoded. The LF line endings of a <code>String</code> body are converted to
CRLF before it is sent.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0563: Vulnerability in lettre</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0563.html</id>

<summary xml:lang="en">Messages for an address with a quoted local part are sent to a different address</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">

</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0563
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Messages for an address with a quoted local part are sent to a different address</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/lettre.html">lettre</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/lettre">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/email.html">#email</a>
<a href="/keywords/address.html">#address</a>
<a href="/keywords/parser.html">#parser</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-62w5-58xq-p27j">GHSA-62w5-58xq-p27j</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/f4d5abc67072c5f35c5cccac6370e94a842cb26c">
https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/f4d5abc67072c5f35c5cccac6370e94a842cb26c
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.11.24</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.11.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A message whose sender or recipient has a quoted local part is sent with a
different address in its envelope than the one the application was given:
<code>&quot;d &quot;@example.com</code> becomes <code>d@example.com</code>. Someone who can choose such an
address can have a message delivered to another mailbox than the one the
application validated.</p>
<p>Addresses without a quoted local part are not affected.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0565: Vulnerability in lettre</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0565.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-18T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0565.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0565: Vulnerability in lettre"/>
<published>2026-11-18T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Unbounded buffering of an SMTP reply allows a malicious server to exhaust memory</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/lettre/RUSTSEC-2026-0565.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/lettre/RUSTSEC-2026-0565.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0565">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0565
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Unbounded buffering of an SMTP reply allows a malicious server to exhaust memory</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-18">
November 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/lettre.html">lettre</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/lettre">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/smtp.html">#smtp</a>
<a href="/keywords/memory.html">#memory</a>
<a href="/keywords/cpu.html">#cpu</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-pmvc-x69f-5p9f">GHSA-pmvc-x69f-5p9f</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/075849fb9f2c956202aaaf2ff73e41f995d02f35">
https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/075849fb9f2c956202aaaf2ff73e41f995d02f35
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.11.24</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A malicious or compromised server can send a reply line that never ends, which
the client buffers until memory runs out. A reply made of many short lines also
keeps a CPU core busy: for a few seconds per reply since 0.11.22, for as long
as the server wants before it.</p>
<p>Applications that connect only to a trusted server over implicit TLS are not
exposed; the risk applies to clients that may connect to untrusted servers, or
that use <code>STARTTLS</code> or no TLS, where a man-in-the-middle can forge the reply.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0560: Vulnerability in lettre</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0560.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-18T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="crypto-failure"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0560.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0560: Vulnerability in lettre"/>
<published>2026-11-18T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">STARTTLS response injection allows a man-in-the-middle to forge SMTP replies</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/lettre/RUSTSEC-2026-0560.md">History</a> ⋅

<h1>

</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>STARTTLS response injection allows a man-in-the-middle to forge SMTP replies</p>

<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>

<time datetime="2026-11-18">

</time>

<dt id="package">Package</dt>

</dd>

</li>

<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>

<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>

<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

<a href="https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/b404122f1b51cdfa32662661849aa3a22e02af38">
https://github.com/lettre/lettre/commit/b404122f1b51cdfa32662661849aa3a22e02af38
</a>
</li>
</ul>

<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>4.8 <span class="tag medium">
MEDIUM
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Scope</dt>
<dd>Unchanged</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:L/A:L">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:L/A:L</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.11.24</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A man-in-the-middle can append replies of their own to the cleartext reply to
<code>STARTTLS</code>. The client takes them for the server's replies to the commands it
then sends over TLS, so the attacker can, for example, make a message that the
server rejected look accepted.</p>
<p>Only connections upgraded with <code>STARTTLS</code> are affected (<code>Tls::Required</code> and
<code>Tls::Opportunistic</code>, as set up by <code>starttls_relay</code>). Implicit TLS
(<code>Tls::Wrapper</code>, as set up by <code>relay</code>) is not.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<p>The chrono crate is unmaintained; all versions are affected.</p>
<p>Recommended alternatives:</p>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://crates.io/crates/jiff">jiff</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://crates.io/crates/time">time</a>
</li>
</ul>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>zlib versions 1.3.1.2 through 1.3.2 contain a heap buffer overflow vulnerability in the gz_vacate() function when processing non-blocking gzwrite() operations with stale external buffer pointers. Attackers can trigger the overflow by calling gzprintf() or gzvprintf() after a write stall, causing an unchecked memmove() to write beyond the internal input buffer boundary.</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0546: Vulnerability in zip</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0546.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0546.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0546: Vulnerability in zip"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">`ZipArchive::extract` can write outside the destination through absolute or chained symlink targets</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/zip/RUSTSEC-2026-0546.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/zip/RUSTSEC-2026-0546.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0546">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0546
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>
<code>ZipArchive::extract</code> can write outside the destination through absolute or chained symlink targets</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-10-22">
October 22, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/zip.html">zip</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/zip">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/symlink.html">#symlink</a>
<a href="/keywords/path-traversal.html">#path-traversal</a>
<a href="/keywords/extraction.html">#extraction</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-115908">CVE-2026-115908</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-hjqx-j4rw-g7xv">GHSA-hjqx-j4rw-g7xv</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/09c030eab56e8db8fb3c1c33f554407b30bad8fa">
https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/09c030eab56e8db8fb3c1c33f554407b30bad8fa
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>7.4 <span class="tag high">
HIGH
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Scope</dt>
<dd>Unchanged</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:H">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:H</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=9.0.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;7.2.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Before writing an entry whose path goes through a symlink, <code>ZipArchive::extract</code> checks the symlink's target against the destination directory. That check compares the paths as text and does not follow symlinks, and from 7.2.0 it drops the root and prefix of an absolute target before comparing. Symlinks taken from the archive were created without any check of their targets.</p>
<p>A crafted archive can first create a symlink that points outside the destination, then a second symlink whose target looks like a path inside the destination but goes through the first one, and then a file entry under the second symlink. The file is written outside the destination directory, for example over an existing file in the user's home directory, and <code>extract</code> still returns <code>Ok(())</code>. On Windows, a target on another drive or share whose path otherwise matches the destination passes the check directly (creating symlinks there needs Developer Mode or administrator rights). The attacker has to know or guess the absolute path of the destination directory.</p>
<p>This affects <code>ZipArchive::extract</code> and <code>ZipArchive::extract_unwrapped_root_dir</code>.</p>
<p>The fix resolves symlink targets one component at a time from the canonicalized destination, following symlinks on the way, and refuses to create a symlink, or write through one, that leads outside the destination.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0548: Vulnerability in zip</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0548.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0548.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0548: Vulnerability in zip"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">`ZipStreamReader` extracts entries under local-header names that can differ from the central directory</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/zip/RUSTSEC-2026-0548.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/zip/RUSTSEC-2026-0548.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0548">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0548
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>
<code>ZipStreamReader</code> extracts entries under local-header names that can differ from the central directory</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-10-17">
October 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/zip.html">zip</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/zip">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/parser-differential.html">#parser-differential</a>
<a href="/keywords/local-file-header.html">#local-file-header</a>
<a href="/keywords/central-directory.html">#central-directory</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-115905">CVE-2026-115905</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-m665-v7pm-7grj">GHSA-m665-v7pm-7grj</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/8068ab480ccf8daa7411424e365fcdbe6d09443e">
https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/8068ab480ccf8daa7411424e365fcdbe6d09443e
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=9.0.5</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A ZIP archive names each entry twice: in the local file header in front of the entry's data, and in the central directory at the end of the archive. <code>ZipArchive</code> lists and opens entries by their central-directory names. <code>ZipStreamReader::visit</code> and <code>ZipStreamReader::extract</code>, like <code>read_zipfile_from_stream</code>, read the local headers in order and use the local-header names without comparing them with the central directory.</p>
<p>An archive can therefore show one set of file names to code that inspects it with <code>ZipArchive</code> and write files under other names when it is extracted with <code>ZipStreamReader</code>. If an application checks an archive one way and extracts it the other way, an attacker can get files written or overwritten inside the destination directory that the check never saw.</p>
<p>The fix adds an opt-in check. <code>ZipStreamReader::enforce_header_consistency(true)</code> compares each local header with its central-directory entry (name, sizes, CRC-32, compression method, flags and modification time) and fails extraction on a mismatch; <code>ZipReadOptions::enforce_header_consistency(true)</code> and <code>ZipArchive::validate_header_consistency()</code> do the same for <code>ZipArchive</code>. The check is off by default, so upgrading alone does not change what <code>ZipStreamReader::new(...)</code> extracts: applications that extract untrusted archives with <code>ZipStreamReader</code> need to turn it on.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0545: Vulnerability in zip</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0545.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="crypto-failure"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0545.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0545: Vulnerability in zip"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">AES entries returned without checking their authentication code when the data ends early or is empty</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/zip/RUSTSEC-2026-0545.md">History</a> ⋅

</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0545
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>AES entries returned without checking their authentication code when the data ends early or is empty</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-10-22">
October 22, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>

</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>

<dd>

</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

<a href="/categories/crypto-failure.html">crypto-failure</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/aes.html">#aes</a>
<a href="/keywords/hmac.html">#hmac</a>
<a href="/keywords/authentication.html">#authentication</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-115906">CVE-2026-115906</a>

<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-8hxf-qr8p-62x4">GHSA-8hxf-qr8p-62x4</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

<a href="https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/ec265db0b0602f8ffb9479327397bddefb7e2216">
https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/ec265db0b0602f8ffb9479327397bddefb7e2216
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>6.5 <span class="tag medium">
MEDIUM
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>Required</dd>
<dt>Scope</dt>
<dd>Unchanged</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:R/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:N">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:R/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:N</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=9.0.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.6.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>For WinZip AES (AE-1 and AE-2) entries, the 10-byte HMAC authentication code was read and checked only after the reader had consumed exactly the entry's declared compressed size. If the archive ended before that size was reached, the read returned end-of-stream and the truncated plaintext was accepted without the check. The authentication code of an entry with no encrypted data was never checked.</p>
<p>AES-CTR ciphertext can be changed bit by bit without the password, and AE-2 entries carry no CRC-32, so an attacker who can modify an archive can alter the decrypted contents of such an entry without any error being reported.</p>
<p>This affects reading AES-encrypted entries with a password (for example <code>ZipArchive::by_name_decrypt</code>) with the <code>aes-crypto</code> feature.</p>
<p>The fix returns an error when the data ends before the declared size, and checks the authentication code of empty entries.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-9hj8-73fh-9963">GHSA-9hj8-73fh-9963</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/pull/991">
https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/pull/991
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>7.5 <span class="tag high">
HIGH
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Scope</dt>
<dd>Unchanged</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=9.0.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;5.1.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>With the <code>legacy-zip</code> feature, the decoders for the Shrink, Reduce and Implode compression methods reserved output capacity for the entry's declared uncompressed size before decoding anything. That size comes from the archive, and a ZIP64 entry can declare any 64-bit value, so reading or extracting a crafted entry of a few bytes panics with &quot;capacity overflow&quot; or fails a huge allocation.</p>
<p>An attacker who can supply an archive can stop a process that reads it, for example through <code>ZipArchive::extract</code>.</p>
<p>Only builds with the <code>legacy-zip</code> feature are affected; it is not enabled by default.</p>
<p>The fix caps the preallocation at 1 MiB, and such an entry now returns an error.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0549: Vulnerability in hickory-proto</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0549.html</id>

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0549
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>NSEC3 NODATA at a zone apex accepted without an NSEC3 record matching the query name (hickory-proto)</p>

<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>

<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/ffdfb295d1abaad42df126c8cdd924cac74b81c0">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/ffdfb295d1abaad42df126c8cdd924cac74b81c0
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
no patched versions
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.25.0-alpha.3</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>In hickory-proto 0.25.0-alpha.3 through 0.26.0-alpha.1, NSEC3 NODATA validation returned a secure result for a query at the zone apex even when no NSEC3 record in the response matched the hash of the query name, which RFC 5155 section 8.5 requires. Someone who can answer or modify DNS responses could replay any genuine signed NSEC3 record from the zone with the zone's signed SOA record and get a denial of any record type at the apex accepted as secure.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in hickory-proto 0.25 or 0.26.0-alpha.1 (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0551: Vulnerability in hickory-proto</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0551.html</id>

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0551
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Records with a DNS class other than the query's marked secure without being validated (hickory-proto)</p>
</span>

<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>

<time datetime="2026-09-03">

<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/d5349ba13a2bd356239c39a6cc9caa10abe06c55">

</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
no patched versions
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Before 0.26.0-beta.1, hickory-proto's DNSSEC validation ignored the DNS class when grouping records into RRsets and left records of a class other than IN out of signature checking, then marked them secure if the IN records validated. Someone who can answer or modify DNS responses could add forged records of another class, including a DS record, and have them returned as validated.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in older hickory-proto releases (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>

license.

<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Up to 0.26.0-alpha.1, hickory-proto's DNSSEC validation kept an NSEC or NSEC3 record for a nonexistence proof if any authority record with the same owner name had validated, not only if that record itself had. Someone who can answer or modify DNS responses could pair a correctly signed record with a forged NSEC or NSEC3 record of the same owner name and get a forged denial accepted as secure.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in older hickory-proto releases (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0552: Vulnerability in actix-http</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0552.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0552.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0552: Vulnerability in actix-http"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Bare LF in chunked trailers can consume pipelined requests or stall body decoding</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/actix-http/RUSTSEC-2026-0552.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0552
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Bare LF in chunked trailers can consume pipelined requests or stall body decoding</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-16">
November 16, 2026
</time>

</li>

<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/actix/actix-web/releases/tag/actix-http-v3.21.1">
https://github.com/actix/actix-web/releases/tag/actix-http-v3.21.1

</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/actix/actix-web/security/advisories/GHSA-9w4r-xc43-v236">
https://github.com/actix/actix-web/security/advisories/GHSA-9w4r-xc43-v236

<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>6.3 <span class="tag medium">
MEDIUM
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Attack Requirements</dt>
<dd>Present</dd>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact to the Subsequent System</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact to the Subsequent System</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact to the Subsequent System</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact to the Vulnerable System</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact to the Vulnerable System</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact to the Vulnerable System</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:L/VI:L/VA:L/SC:N/SI:N/SA:N">CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:L/VI:L/VA:L/SC:N/SI:N/SA:N</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=3.21.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Affected versions of <code>actix-http</code> skip a bare line feed (LF) inside an HTTP/1.1
chunked trailer instead of rejecting the invalid line ending. Trailer lines
must end with a carriage return and line feed (CRLF).</p>
<p>For example, after <code>0\r\nX\n</code>, the parser continues reading trailer data. If a
request follows on the same connection, its head can be consumed as part of the
trailer, so that request is never dispatched. If no more bytes arrive, body
decoding waits for more input instead of reporting an error. A remote client
can trigger these effects without authentication.</p>
<p>Request smuggling additionally requires an HTTP intermediary that forwards the
malformed body but uses a different request boundary. The tested HTTP
intermediaries either rejected the input or consumed and normalized it before
forwarding. Proxy access-control bypass, cache poisoning, and effects on other
users were not demonstrated.</p>
<p>Upgrade to <code>actix-http</code> 3.21.1 or later. The fix rejects bare LF and invalid
control bytes in trailers before consuming the following request, while
preserving partial reads and valid CRLF-terminated trailers.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC-BY-4.0.html">CC-BY-4.0</a>
license.
Source: <a href="https://github.com/actix/actix-web/releases/tag/actix-http-v3.21.1">https://github.com/actix/actix-web/releases/tag/actix-http-v3.21.1</a>
</p>
</article>
</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0553: Vulnerability in actix-http</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0553.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0553.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0553: Vulnerability in actix-http"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Malformed quoted chunk extensions can end HTTP/1.1 request bodies early</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/actix-http/RUSTSEC-2026-0553.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0553
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Malformed quoted chunk extensions can end HTTP/1.1 request bodies early</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-16">
November 16, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/actix-http.html">actix-http</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/actix-http">crates.io</a>)
</dd>

</dd>

<a href="https://github.com/actix/actix-web/security/advisories/GHSA-9543-cj4x-h76q">
https://github.com/actix/actix-web/security/advisories/GHSA-9543-cj4x-h76q
</a>
</li>
</ul>

<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>6.3 <span class="tag medium">
MEDIUM
</span>
</dd>

</dl>

<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:N/VI:L/VA:N/SC:N/SI:N/SA:N">CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/AT:P/PR:N/UI:N/VC:N/VI:L/VA:N/SC:N/SI:N/SA:N</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=3.21.3</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Affected versions of <code>actix-http</code> do not track quoted strings when parsing
HTTP/1.1 chunk extensions. A carriage return inside a quoted extension value
is treated as the end of the chunk-size line, even if the closing quote is
missing. For example, <code>0;x=&quot;\r\n\r\n</code> is accepted as the end of a chunked body,
and a following pipelined request is dispatched on the same connection.</p>
<p>A remote client can trigger this without authentication. CR and LF are invalid
inside HTTP quoted strings, including after a backslash; they must be rejected
rather than interpreted as framing or accepted as quoted data.</p>
<p>Request smuggling requires an upstream intermediary that forwards the malformed
input while using a different request boundary. Dispatching two requests on the
sender's own connection does not by itself demonstrate an authorization bypass
or an effect on another user. Proxy bypass, cache poisoning, and cross-user
response mixing were not demonstrated by the reproduction.</p>
<p>Upgrade to <code>actix-http</code> 3.21.3 or later. The fix preserves quote and escape state
across reads and rejects invalid control bytes inside quoted strings and quoted
pairs before consuming the following body or request bytes.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC-BY-4.0.html">CC-BY-4.0</a>
license.
Source: <a href="https://github.com/actix/actix-web/releases/tag/actix-http-v3.21.3">https://github.com/actix/actix-web/releases/tag/actix-http-v3.21.3</a>
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0554: Vulnerability in actix-http</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0554.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0554.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0554: Vulnerability in actix-http"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Partial requests on keep-alive connections can bypass the request header timeout</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0554
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Partial requests on keep-alive connections can bypass the request header timeout</p>
</span>

</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Affected versions of <code>actix-http</code> apply the request header timeout only to the
first request on an HTTP/1 connection. When data arrives for a later request,
the dispatcher clears the idle keep-alive timer without starting a request
header timer.</p>
<p>A remote client without authentication can complete one request, send one byte
of a second request, and leave the connection open indefinitely without sending
more data. The same problem occurs when a complete pipelined request is followed
by a partial request in the same read. Repeating this can exhaust connection or
file-descriptor limits and prevent new connections from being accepted. The
effect depends on the server's resource limits and upstream connection controls.</p>
<p>Upgrade to <code>actix-http</code> 3.21.2 or later. The fix applies the configured request
header timeout to later partial requests, including those left in the buffer
after a complete pipelined request. Additional bytes do not renew the deadline.
The timeout covers request headers, not upload bodies.</p>
<p>If an upgrade is not possible, disable HTTP keep-alive, for example with
<code>HttpServer::keep_alive(KeepAlive::Disabled)</code> in <code>actix-web</code>. Connection limits
can reduce resource use but do not make stalled connections expire.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC-BY-4.0.html">CC-BY-4.0</a>
license.
Source: <a href="https://github.com/actix/actix-web/releases/tag/actix-http-v3.21.2">https://github.com/actix/actix-web/releases/tag/actix-http-v3.21.2</a>
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0557: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0557.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0557.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0557: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Unsigned NSEC or NSEC3 record used in a nonexistence proof when another record at the same name is signed</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0557.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0557
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Unsigned NSEC or NSEC3 record used in a nonexistence proof when another record at the same name is signed</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec.html">#nsec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec3.html">#nsec3</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123590">CVE-2026-123590</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-57pw-897j-v4j6">GHSA-57pw-897j-v4j6</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/68cb330eb7e73a7db0cec9a25888db9f4bcf2086">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/68cb330eb7e73a7db0cec9a25888db9f4bcf2086
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When DNSSEC validation collected the NSEC and NSEC3 records from a response's authority section to check a denial of existence, it kept a record if any authority record with the same owner name had validated, not only if that record itself had. A response could therefore pair a correctly signed record with a forged NSEC or NSEC3 record of the same owner name, and the forged record was used in the nonexistence proof.</p>
<p>An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can use this to get a forged NXDOMAIN or NODATA answer accepted as secure.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix keeps an NSEC or NSEC3 record only when its own signature validated.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<entry>
<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0556: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0556.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0556.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0556: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">NSEC3 NODATA at a zone apex accepted without an NSEC3 record matching the query name</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0556.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0556.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0556">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0556
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>NSEC3 NODATA at a zone apex accepted without an NSEC3 record matching the query name</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec3.html">#nsec3</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123600">CVE-2026-123600</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-3jvh-8vj5-65rq">GHSA-3jvh-8vj5-65rq</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/ffdfb295d1abaad42df126c8cdd924cac74b81c0">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/ffdfb295d1abaad42df126c8cdd924cac74b81c0
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>RFC 5155 section 8.5 says a NODATA response proven with NSEC3 needs an NSEC3 record whose hashed owner name equals the hash of the query name, and whose type bitmap has neither the query type nor CNAME.</p>
<p>NSEC3 NODATA validation skipped this when the query name was the zone apex (the SOA owner). If no NSEC3 record matched the query name, no closest encloser was found and no wildcard applied, it still returned a secure result.</p>
<p>An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can replay any genuine signed NSEC3 record from the zone together with the zone's signed SOA record, and get a NODATA answer for any record type at the apex accepted as secure. A recursor caches that denial for the negative TTL.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix treats such a response as bogus.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>

<entry>
<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0558: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0558.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0558.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0558: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-17T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Records with a DNS class other than the query&apos;s marked secure without being validated</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0558.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0558.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0558">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0558
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Records with a DNS class other than the query's marked secure without being validated</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-17">
November 17, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/class.html">#class</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123599">CVE-2026-123599</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wjgj-fvg9-65w9">GHSA-wjgj-fvg9-65w9</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/d5349ba13a2bd356239c39a6cc9caa10abe06c55">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/d5349ba13a2bd356239c39a6cc9caa10abe06c55
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When DNSSEC validation grouped a response's records into RRsets, it did not take the DNS class into account, and records of a class other than IN were left out before signatures were checked. If the IN records validated, the records of other classes (such as CH or HS) were marked secure too.</p>
<p>An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can add forged records of another class to a response and have them returned as validated. A forged DS record of another class can also get past validation, which lets any DNSKEY for the child zone be accepted and any answer from that zone be forged. How much this matters depends on how the application treats records of an unexpected class.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix rejects a whole response if any record in its answer, authority or additional section has a class different from the query's.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0524: Vulnerability in quinn-proto</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0524.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0524.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0524: Vulnerability in quinn-proto"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Long Retry token can panic or exhaust memory in a QUIC client</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/quinn-proto/RUSTSEC-2026-0524.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/quinn-proto/RUSTSEC-2026-0524.md">Edit</a> ⋅

</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0524
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Long Retry token can panic or exhaust memory in a QUIC client</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-30">
September 30, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/quinn-proto.html">quinn-proto</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/quinn-proto">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/quic.html">#quic</a>
<a href="/keywords/panic.html">#panic</a>
<a href="/keywords/oom.html">#oom</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wppq-2f6r-wfvm">GHSA-wppq-2f6r-wfvm</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/3bba467893ceff64d6cda0b3a2c51a1429e10372">
https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/3bba467893ceff64d6cda0b3a2c51a1429e10372
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/d45b5136ed1d5b2b04ff6456443c0662acf1d0d3">
https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/d45b5136ed1d5b2b04ff6456443c0662acf1d0d3
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/74c33ff75281d7751488e410e21e4f030782b655">
https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/74c33ff75281d7751488e410e21e4f030782b655
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/a6e78d1c8bf28630f1e92fbcc38f60e0a134b01a">
https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/a6e78d1c8bf28630f1e92fbcc38f60e0a134b01a
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.11.19</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.11.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A client kept the token from a Retry packet without any length limit and put it into the header of every Initial packet it sent afterwards. Initial datagrams are 1200 bytes. When the token and connection IDs left no room for the minimum payload, <code>PacketBuilder::new</code> computed a minimum packet size larger than the maximum, which only a debug assertion checked. On the next <code>poll_transmit</code>, debug builds panic, and release builds try to allocate more than 10 GiB in one call. With longer tokens the allocation fails and the process aborts.</p>
<p>A matching Retry packet can be built from the client's first Initial alone. The attacker can therefore be the server being contacted, or anyone who sees that Initial and can answer before the server does. Every application that makes outgoing connections with <code>quinn-proto</code> is affected, including all <code>quinn</code> clients. Servers do not process Retry packets.</p>
<p>The fix closes the connection with <code>PROTOCOL_VIOLATION</code> when an Initial token leaves too little room for frames. That covers Retry tokens and cached address-validation tokens, since both come from the peer. It also reserves room for CONNECTION_CLOSE after large Initial headers. Both changes are in 0.11.19.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
