<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0523: Vulnerability in quinn-proto</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0523.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0523.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0523: Vulnerability in quinn-proto"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">MAX_STREAM_DATA frame can open streams beyond the stream limit</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/quinn-proto/RUSTSEC-2026-0523.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/quinn-proto/RUSTSEC-2026-0523.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0523">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0523
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>MAX_STREAM_DATA frame can open streams beyond the stream limit</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-30">
September 30, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/quinn-proto.html">quinn-proto</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/quinn-proto">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/quic.html">#quic</a>
<a href="/keywords/stream-limit.html">#stream-limit</a>
<a href="/keywords/oom.html">#oom</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-53rm-773f-4q8c">GHSA-53rm-773f-4q8c</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/3eb7465d9e11d7a245e99cde1a4eb872ec26d264">
https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/3eb7465d9e11d7a245e99cde1a4eb872ec26d264
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/73114d8289d42bd536c790fa00a9beed05a4e5fa">
https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/73114d8289d42bd536c790fa00a9beed05a4e5fa
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.11.19</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.11.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When a MAX_STREAM_DATA frame named a peer-initiated bidirectional stream that was not open yet, <code>received_max_stream_data</code> treated the stream, and every lower-numbered stream, as opened without checking the stream ID against the limit advertised to the peer. RFC 9000 section 4.6 requires closing the connection with <code>STREAM_LIMIT_ERROR</code> in this case, and the STREAM and RESET_STREAM handlers already did so.</p>
<p>After the handshake, a peer can send one small MAX_STREAM_DATA frame naming a very large stream index, and <code>accept</code> then yields every stream up to that index. In <code>quinn</code>, a loop that keeps calling <code>accept_bi</code> can be handed more than 2^40 streams. Depending on the runtime, the endpoint runs out of memory, stops accepting new connections, or keeps busy indefinitely, even after the peer has gone away. Clients and servers that accept peer-initiated bidirectional streams are affected. Unidirectional streams are not.</p>
<p>The fix validates the stream ID in <code>received_max_stream_data</code> and adds a defensive check where streams are opened implicitly. It is in 0.11.19.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>
<entry>
<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0525: Vulnerability in libp2p-quic</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0525.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0525.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0525: Vulnerability in libp2p-quic"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Remote panic when a peer certificate expires during the QUIC handshake</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/libp2p-quic/RUSTSEC-2026-0525.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/libp2p-quic/RUSTSEC-2026-0525.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0525">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0525
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Remote panic when a peer certificate expires during the QUIC handshake</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-07-10">
July 10, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/libp2p-quic.html">libp2p-quic</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/libp2p-quic">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/libp2p.html">#libp2p</a>
<a href="/keywords/quic.html">#quic</a>
<a href="/keywords/tls.html">#tls</a>
<a href="/keywords/panic.html">#panic</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-61544">CVE-2026-61544</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5hq8-qhww-jm7q">GHSA-5hq8-qhww-jm7q</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/212f3774af048e2cecfb2e6b1e08477685e52b22">
https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/212f3774af048e2cecfb2e6b1e08477685e52b22
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/pull/6525">
https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/pull/6525
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.13.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>After the QUIC handshake finished, the peer's libp2p TLS certificate was parsed a second time to get its peer ID, and the result was unwrapped with <code>expect</code>, on the assumption that it could not fail because the certificate had already been validated. Parsing checks the certificate's validity period again against the current time. A remote peer can present a certificate that is about to expire and delay the end of the handshake until it has expired, so the second parse fails and the process panics. No authentication is needed; any application with a reachable QUIC listener is affected.</p>
<p>The fix returns a connection error instead of panicking when the peer identity or certificate cannot be read after the handshake. It is in 0.13.1, and was backported to the main branch in libp2p/rust-libp2p#6525 for 0.14.0.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/8fde2dc0fae8b433f97c6cdf9ee24f59d51a359c
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>7.5 <span class="tag high">
HIGH
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Scope</dt>
<dd>Unchanged</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.17.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>The rendezvous server's registration store did not limit how many namespaces a single peer could register, or how many registrations it held in total. Each registration stays until its TTL expires, up to 72 hours, and is not removed when the peer disconnects. An unauthenticated peer can send REGISTER requests for new namespaces in a loop, and many peers together can exhaust the server's memory, cutting off discovery for every client that relies on it.</p>
<p>The fix adds limits on registrations per peer (32 by default) and in total (10,000 by default), configurable through <code>Config</code>, and answers requests over the limit with an <code>Unavailable</code> error. It is in 0.17.1.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.17.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Each DISCOVER request to a rendezvous server creates a pagination cookie, which the server kept in a map together with the registrations already returned for it. Entries were never evicted or capped, so an unauthenticated peer sending ordinary DISCOVER requests can make the server's memory grow without bound.</p>
<p>The fix keeps cookies in a fixed-size LRU cache (10,000 entries by default, configurable through <code>Config</code>). It is in 0.17.1.</p>

<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">UB can be reached from safe code</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/ref-swap/RUSTSEC-2026-0528.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0528
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>UB can be reached from safe code</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/ref-swap.html">ref-swap</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/ref-swap">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
<span class="tag info">INFO</span>
Unsound
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/memory-corruption.html">memory-corruption</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/use-after-free.html">#use-after-free</a>
<a href="/keywords/null-pointer.html">#null-pointer</a>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/Nitrokey/ref-swap/releases/tag/v0.1.3">
https://github.com/Nitrokey/ref-swap/releases/tag/v0.1.3
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.1.3</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>The variance of <code>RefSwap</code> and <code>OptionRefSwap</code> were incorrect, possibly allowing use-after-free.</p>
<p>
<code>OptionRefSwap::into_inner</code> did not check for null.</p>
<p>The fix is to make <code>RefSwap</code> and <code>OptionRefSwap</code> invariant, and to change <code>OptionRefSwap::into_inner</code> to return an option.
It is available in version 0.1.3.
If your project still compiles with 0.1.3 then it is not affected by the unsoundness on version 0.1.2</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0529: Vulnerability in bsv-rs</title>

</ul>

<dl>
<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>bsv_rs::kvstore::KVStoreInterpreter::verify_signature_legacy</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.2.0, &#60;0.8.9</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.0-beta.1, &#60;=0.9.0-beta.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>
<code>KVStoreInterpreter::verify_signature_legacy(&amp;PushDrop, &amp;PublicKey)</code>, public
and deprecated since 0.2.0 (the crate's first release), read neither argument
and answered <code>true</code>. A KVStore token signed by nobody, a signed token checked
against a key other than its controller's, and a PushDrop with no fields each
passed. Its documentation said it delegated to the <code>KVStoreFields</code>-based
verifier; it did not.</p>
<p>An application that checks, with this function, a KVStore token it did not
write (an overlay lookup's answer, or a transaction output a peer hands it) and
acts on <code>true</code> accepts a value no controller signed, attributed to whatever
controller the token names. No caller in the crate itself uses the function.
This was run on 0.2.0, 0.5.0, 0.8.0, 0.8.7, 0.8.8, 0.9.0-beta.1 and
0.9.0-beta.7 as crates.io serves them, and the source of all 63 published
versions carries the same constant body (the yanked 0.3.21 included).</p>
<p>0.8.9 answers <code>true</code> only for a token whose controller field is the
given key and whose last field is that key's signature over the others (the
0.9 line's next prerelease carries the same fix). There is no workaround:
upgrade to 0.8.9, or, on the 0.9 line, to its next prerelease.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0531: Vulnerability in hickory-proto</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0531.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0531.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0531: Vulnerability in hickory-proto"/>

<summary xml:lang="en">Child-side NSEC or NSEC3 record accepted as proof that a delegation has no DS record (hickory-proto)</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-proto/RUSTSEC-2026-0531.md">History</a> ⋅

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0531

<span class="subtitle">

</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Up to 0.26.0-alpha.1, hickory-proto's DNSSEC validation accepted an NSEC or NSEC3 record from the child side of a zone cut, which has the SOA bit set, as proof that the parent has no DS record for the delegation (RFC 4035 section 5.2, RFC 6840 section 4.4). Someone who can answer or modify DNS responses could make a secure delegation look insecure and then forge records in the child zone.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in older hickory-proto releases (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<entry>
<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0530: Vulnerability in hickory-proto</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0530.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0530.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0530: Vulnerability in hickory-proto"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Parent-side NSEC at a zone cut accepted as proof of nonexistence (hickory-proto)</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0530
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Parent-side NSEC at a zone cut accepted as proof of nonexistence (hickory-proto)</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-proto.html">hickory-proto</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-proto">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec.html">#nsec</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123596">CVE-2026-123596</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-3r6v-f3jh-vvqm">GHSA-3r6v-f3jh-vvqm</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/2e3c829ea45000b1c409a64a03bd422618372471">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/2e3c829ea45000b1c409a64a03bd422618372471
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
no patched versions
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Up to 0.26.0-alpha.1, hickory-proto's DNSSEC validation accepted the NSEC record that a parent zone signs at a delegation as proof that names below the zone cut, or other types at the cut, do not exist. Such a record only proves that no DS record exists there (RFC 6840 section 4.1). Someone who can answer or modify DNS responses could replay the parent's genuine signed records to get a denial for an existing name in the child zone accepted as secure.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in older hickory-proto releases (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0532: Vulnerability in hickory-proto</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0532.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>

<summary xml:lang="en">Parent-side NSEC3 at a zone cut accepted as proof of nonexistence (hickory-proto 0.25)</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0532
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Parent-side NSEC3 at a zone cut accepted as proof of nonexistence (hickory-proto 0.25)</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-proto.html">hickory-proto</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-proto">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec3.html">#nsec3</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123595">CVE-2026-123595</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-624w-vvww-xvpw">GHSA-624w-vvww-xvpw</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/889801333d7f18d59a4468100e8cd1859e083d43">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/889801333d7f18d59a4468100e8cd1859e083d43
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
no patched versions
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.25.0</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>In hickory-proto 0.25.0 through 0.26.0-alpha.1, NSEC3 validation picked the closest encloser by hashed owner name alone and accepted the NSEC3 record that a parent zone signs at a delegation as proof that names at or below the zone cut do not exist. Such a record only proves that no DS record exists there (RFC 6840 section 4.1, RFC 5155 section 8.3). Someone who can answer or modify DNS responses could replay the parent's genuine signed records to get a denial for an existing name in the child zone accepted as secure.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in hickory-proto 0.25 (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor 0.25) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0533: Vulnerability in ammonia</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0533.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="format-injection"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0533.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0533: Vulnerability in ammonia"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Incorrect handling of embedded SVG and MathML leads to mutation XSS</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/ammonia/RUSTSEC-2026-0533.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/ammonia/RUSTSEC-2026-0533.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0533">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0533
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Incorrect handling of embedded SVG and MathML leads to mutation XSS</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/ammonia.html">ammonia</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/ammonia">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/format-injection.html">format-injection</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/html.html">#html</a>
<a href="/keywords/xss.html">#xss</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-934p-vp7m-gx3c">GHSA-934p-vp7m-gx3c</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/rust-ammonia/ammonia/pull/269">
https://github.com/rust-ammonia/ammonia/pull/269
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=4.2.5</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=4.1.9, &#60;4.2.0</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=3.3.5, &#60;4.0.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>The MathML text integration points (<code>mi</code>, <code>mo</code>, <code>mn</code>, <code>ms</code>, <code>mtext</code>) and SVG <code>foreignObject</code> can be made to contain a tag in the other one's namespace (a MathML integration point with a child in the SVG namespace, or vice versa) without the root tag that switches out of the HTML namespace. If the attacker can construct input that parses differently in as HTML than as foreign XML, they can inject arbitrary JavaScript code into the user's browser.</p>
<p>For example:</p>
<ul>
<li>The user writes <code>&lt;math&gt;&lt;mi&gt;&lt;svg&gt;&lt;gadget&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;&lt;/mi&gt;&lt;/math&gt;</code>.</li>
<li>The application enables <code>math</code>, <code>mi</code>, and the gadget, but not <code>svg</code>.</li>
<li>Ammonia filters the input to <code>&lt;math&gt;&lt;mi&gt;&lt;gadget&gt;&lt;/mi&gt;&lt;/math&gt;</code>.</li>
<li>Because svg is now gone, the gadget switches from being parsed as SVG to being parsed as HTML.</li>
<li>The gadget is written in such a way that it exploits the parsing differences between HTML and MathML.</li>
</ul>
<p>As far as we know, the gadget can only be written using a tag that is parsed as raw text in HTML. These <a href="https://github.com/servo/html5ever/blob/a027c2835472d518893c906dc29c42200af3c960/html5ever/src/tree_builder/rules.rs">elements</a> are:</p>
<ul>
<li>title</li>
<li>textarea</li>
<li>xmp</li>
<li>iframe</li>
<li>noembed</li>
<li>noframes</li>
<li>plaintext</li>
<li>noscript</li>
<li>style</li>
<li>script</li>
</ul>
<p>Applications that do not explicitly allow any of these tags should not be affected, since none are allowed by default.</p>
<hr />
<p>This vulnerability was reported by Roger Meyer.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0536: Vulnerability in libp2p-gossipsub</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0536.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0536.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0536: Vulnerability in libp2p-gossipsub"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Unbounded memory growth from GRAFT messages for unsubscribed topics</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/libp2p-gossipsub/RUSTSEC-2026-0536.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/libp2p-gossipsub/RUSTSEC-2026-0536.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0536">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0536
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Unbounded memory growth from GRAFT messages for unsubscribed topics</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-05">
September 5, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/libp2p-gossipsub.html">libp2p-gossipsub</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/libp2p-gossipsub">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/libp2p.html">#libp2p</a>
<a href="/keywords/gossipsub.html">#gossipsub</a>
<a href="/keywords/memory-exhaustion.html">#memory-exhaustion</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-95859">CVE-2026-95859</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-g3g5-x568-qvqx">GHSA-g3g5-x568-qvqx</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/51ed5f00e1b0620ded3444ecadf18477052148ea">
https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/51ed5f00e1b0620ded3444ecadf18477052148ea
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/pull/6608">
https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/pull/6608
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.49.5</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When a GRAFT control message arrived, every topic it named was added to that peer's set of topics, on the assumption that a peer grafting us must be subscribed. Nothing checked that the peer had subscribed, and subscription filters only apply to SUBSCRIBE messages. A peer can keep sending GRAFTs with new short topic IDs over one connection, and the set grows until the peer unsubscribes or disconnects. Memory grows without bound and the node can be killed for running out of memory.</p>
<p>The fix no longer records topics from GRAFT messages and drops a GRAFT for a topic the peer has not subscribed to. It also makes <code>MaxCountSubscriptionFilter</code> the default subscription filter. It is in 0.49.5, and was backported to the 0.50 line before 0.50.0.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0534: Vulnerability in libp2p-gossipsub</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0534.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0534.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0534: Vulnerability in libp2p-gossipsub"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Unbounded backoff state from PRUNE messages for unknown topics</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/libp2p-gossipsub/RUSTSEC-2026-0534.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/libp2p-gossipsub/RUSTSEC-2026-0534.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0534">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0534
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Unbounded backoff state from PRUNE messages for unknown topics</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-10-19">
October 19, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/libp2p-gossipsub.html">libp2p-gossipsub</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/libp2p-gossipsub">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/libp2p.html">#libp2p</a>
<a href="/keywords/gossipsub.html">#gossipsub</a>
<a href="/keywords/memory-exhaustion.html">#memory-exhaustion</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-vrx6-jcp8-vm7m">GHSA-vrx6-jcp8-vm7m</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/9c647828abea0dfc0b4e827dff1e176ec8b44752">
https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/9c647828abea0dfc0b4e827dff1e176ec8b44752
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/pull/6707">
https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/pull/6707
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.50.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A PRUNE control message names topics and asks for a backoff on each. The handler recorded a backoff for every named topic, including topics the local node had never joined. A peer can send PRUNEs naming many new short topic IDs with a long backoff. Each pair is kept until the backoff expires, up to an hour, even after the peer disconnects, so a stream of PRUNEs, possibly from many peer IDs, can use up the node's memory. Peer scoring does not penalise these messages.</p>
<p>The fix ignores PRUNE entries for topics that are not in the node's mesh. It is in 0.50.1, and was backported to the main branch in libp2p/rust-libp2p#6707.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<entry>
<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0535: Vulnerability in libp2p-gossipsub</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0535.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0535.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0535: Vulnerability in libp2p-gossipsub"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Remote panic from an oversized PRUNE backoff</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/libp2p-gossipsub/RUSTSEC-2026-0535.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/libp2p-gossipsub/RUSTSEC-2026-0535.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0535">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0535
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Remote panic from an oversized PRUNE backoff</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-03-18">
March 18, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/libp2p-gossipsub.html">libp2p-gossipsub</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/libp2p-gossipsub">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/libp2p.html">#libp2p</a>
<a href="/keywords/gossipsub.html">#gossipsub</a>
<a href="/keywords/panic.html">#panic</a>
<a href="/keywords/overflow.html">#overflow</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-33040">CVE-2026-33040</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-gc42-3jg7-rxr2">GHSA-gc42-3jg7-rxr2</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/84153a559bdbcb92a48413dd2a31035800cb882d">
https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/84153a559bdbcb92a48413dd2a31035800cb882d
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.49.3</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When a peer sent a PRUNE control message, the backoff it asked for was turned into an expiry time with <code>Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(backoff)</code>. That addition panics on overflow, so a peer that sends a PRUNE with a very large backoff, such as <code>u64::MAX</code>, crashes the node. No authentication is needed beyond an ordinary libp2p connection with the gossipsub protocol, and the attacker can repeat it after every restart.</p>
<p>The fix uses <code>Instant::checked_add</code> in <code>BackoffStorage::update_backoff</code> and ignores a backoff that would overflow. It is in 0.49.3.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0537: Vulnerability in libp2p-gossipsub</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0537.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>

<summary xml:lang="en">Remote panic in the heartbeat from a large PRUNE backoff</summary>

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0537
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Remote panic in the heartbeat from a large PRUNE backoff</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-03-27">
March 27, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/libp2p-gossipsub.html">libp2p-gossipsub</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/libp2p-gossipsub">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/libp2p.html">#libp2p</a>
<a href="/keywords/gossipsub.html">#gossipsub</a>
<a href="/keywords/panic.html">#panic</a>
<a href="/keywords/overflow.html">#overflow</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-34219">CVE-2026-34219</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-xqmp-fxgv-xvq5">GHSA-xqmp-fxgv-xvq5</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/55e4a64630f66d55fec5955c8f9905413c5d0859">
https://github.com/libp2p/rust-libp2p/commit/55e4a64630f66d55fec5955c8f9905413c5d0859
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.49.4</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>The 0.49.3 fix stopped an overflow when a PRUNE backoff is first stored, but a backoff just small enough to be stored could still overflow later. On each heartbeat, expired backoffs were found with <code>backoff_time + slack &gt; now</code>, which panics when the stored time is close to the largest <code>Instant</code>. A peer that sends one PRUNE with such a backoff crashes the node a heartbeat or so later, and can repeat it after every restart.</p>
<p>The fix caps a remote PRUNE backoff at one hour, uses <code>checked_add</code> for the heartbeat comparison, and replaces other unchecked time arithmetic in the behaviour, peer scoring and time cache with checked or saturating operations. It is in 0.49.4.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0538: Vulnerability in quinn-udp</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0538.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0538.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0538: Vulnerability in quinn-udp"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Receive loop can spin after a truncated datagram</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/quinn-udp/RUSTSEC-2026-0538.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0538
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Receive loop can spin after a truncated datagram</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-30">
September 30, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/quinn-udp.html">quinn-udp</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/quinn-udp">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/udp.html">#udp</a>
<a href="/keywords/infinite-loop.html">#infinite-loop</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-6pp4-4cxf-xf88">GHSA-6pp4-4cxf-xf88</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/462865a6c33039c26076f7077c7cd7e2f997b9cd">
https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/462865a6c33039c26076f7077c7cd7e2f997b9cd
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/6862d3dcc9a9333fc4160481b3e5e5d154d4dd36">
https://github.com/quinn-rs/quinn/commit/6862d3dcc9a9333fc4160481b3e5e5d154d4dd36
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.5.16, &#60;0.6.0</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.6.3</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Some targets read one datagram per <code>recvmsg</code> call instead of using <code>recvmmsg</code>: Apple platforms without the <code>fast-apple-datapath</code> feature, OpenBSD, NetBSD, Solaris and illumos, and from 0.6 also DragonFly BSD, Redox and GNU/Hurd. On these targets the receive loop set up its <code>msghdr</code> only once and looked at the <code>MSG_TRUNC</code> flag before checking whether <code>recvmsg</code> had failed. After a datagram larger than the receive buffer was dropped, a following <code>EAGAIN</code> still saw the old truncation flag. The loop then kept issuing non-blocking receives instead of returning <code>WouldBlock</code>.</p>
<p>When the receive buffer is smaller than a datagram that arrives, a remote sender can keep the task that drives the socket busy, before any QUIC packet authentication. The loop ends only when a later datagram fits in the buffer. Whether an application is exposed therefore depends on the receive buffer size it uses. Linux, Android, FreeBSD and Windows use other receive paths and are not affected.</p>
<p>The fix rebuilds the header before every <code>recvmsg</code> call and returns receive errors before checking the truncation flag. It is in 0.5.16 and 0.6.3.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0540: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0540.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0540.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0540: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Child-side NSEC or NSEC3 record accepted as proof that a delegation has no DS record</summary>

<article>
<span class="floating-menu">

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0540
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Child-side NSEC or NSEC3 record accepted as proof that a delegation has no DS record</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>

<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>

<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>

https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/ec98851f4d175d97b61701bc539523ff63ddbb00
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>5.9 <span class="tag medium">
MEDIUM
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>

<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:N">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:N</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>To show that a delegation is insecure, a validator needs proof that the parent zone has no DS record for it. That proof must come from the parent side of the zone cut: RFC 4035 section 5.2, RFC 5155 section 8.9 and RFC 6840 section 4.4 require the matching NSEC or NSEC3 record not to have the SOA bit set, since a record with SOA comes from the child zone's apex.</p>
<p>DNSSEC validation did not check this. An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can answer a DS query with the child zone's own signed NSEC or NSEC3 record for its apex, which does not list DS, and have the validator accept that the delegation is insecure. Records in the child zone can then be forged without any signature.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix treats a NODATA proof for a DS query as bogus when the directly matching NSEC or NSEC3 record has SOA in its type bitmap. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0541: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0541.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0541.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0541: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Parent-side NSEC3 at a zone cut accepted as proof of nonexistence at and below the cut</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0541.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0541.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0541">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0541
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Parent-side NSEC3 at a zone cut accepted as proof of nonexistence at and below the cut</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec3.html">#nsec3</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123595">CVE-2026-123595</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-624w-vvww-xvpw">GHSA-624w-vvww-xvpw</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/889801333d7f18d59a4468100e8cd1859e083d43">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/889801333d7f18d59a4468100e8cd1859e083d43
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>At a delegation, the parent zone signs an NSEC3 record for the child's name whose type bitmap has NS but not SOA (or has DNAME). RFC 6840 section 4.1 and RFC 5155 section 8.3 say such a record only proves that no DS record exists at that name.</p>
<p>NSEC3 validation picked the closest encloser by matching the hashed owner name alone, without looking at the type bitmap, so it accepted such a record as the closest encloser for names below the cut. For NODATA responses, it accepted a direct match on such a record as proof that any type other than the queried one and CNAME was absent, not only DS. An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can replay the parent's genuine signed records to get an NXDOMAIN for a name below the cut, or a NODATA for a non-DS type at the cut, accepted as secure. A recursor caches that denial for the negative TTL.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix adds <code>NSEC3::is_ancestor_delegation()</code> in hickory-proto. Validation no longer accepts such a record as a closest encloser, and treats a direct match on it as bogus unless the query is for DS. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0539: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0539.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0539.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0539: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-13T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Parent-side NSEC at a zone cut accepted as proof of nonexistence below the cut</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0539.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0539
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Parent-side NSEC at a zone cut accepted as proof of nonexistence below the cut</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-13">
November 13, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec.html">#nsec</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123596">CVE-2026-123596</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-3r6v-f3jh-vvqm">GHSA-3r6v-f3jh-vvqm</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/2e3c829ea45000b1c409a64a03bd422618372471">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/2e3c829ea45000b1c409a64a03bd422618372471
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>At a delegation, the parent zone signs an NSEC record for the child's name whose type bitmap has NS but not SOA (or has DNAME). RFC 6840 section 4.1 says such a record only proves that no DS record exists at that name. It says nothing about names below the cut or about other types at the cut, because those belong to the child zone.</p>
<p>DNSSEC validation did not make this distinction. It used such a record as a covering NSEC for names below the cut, and as a direct match proving that other types did not exist at the cut. An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can replay the parent's genuine signed records to get an NXDOMAIN or NODATA response for an existing name in the child zone accepted as secure. A recursor caches that denial for the negative TTL.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix adds <code>NSEC::is_ancestor_delegation()</code> in hickory-proto. Validation no longer uses such a record to cover a name below its owner, and treats a direct match on it as bogus unless the query is for DS. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<summary xml:lang="en">Memory exhaustion from an unchecked SSH packet length</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">

<span class="subtitle">
<p>Memory exhaustion from an unchecked SSH packet length</p>

</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.44.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When reading a packet, russh took the 4-byte packet length sent by the peer and sized its receive buffer from it without any upper bound. The length is fully controlled by the other side of the connection and is read before authentication, so an unauthenticated client can claim packets of up to about 4 GiB and make a server allocate that much memory per connection. A few such connections are enough to run the process out of memory.</p>
<p>From 0.44.1, lengths above 256 KiB are rejected with <code>Error::PacketSize</code> before the buffer is sized.</p>

license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0494: Vulnerability in russh</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0494.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0494.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0494: Vulnerability in russh"/>

<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>

<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.34.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>

<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Many of russh's client and server message handlers decoded length-prefixed strings, byte fields and name-lists from the peer into owned <code>String</code>, <code>Vec</code> or name-list values first and only checked their size and meaning afterwards. This includes the algorithm name-lists in <code>SSH_MSG_KEXINIT</code>, which a server parses during the initial key exchange, before any authentication.</p>
<p>A peer can fill these fields with oversized or highly fragmented values, for example a name-list of hundreds of thousands of one-letter algorithm names, and make russh allocate far more memory than the packet itself takes. With many concurrent connections this can exhaust the memory of a server process.</p>
<p>From 0.61.0, name-lists are rejected before they are split into separate strings when they are longer than 16 KiB or hold more than 1,024 entries, and the key exchange decodes its algorithm lists through this bounded decoder.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0493: Vulnerability in russh</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0493.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0493.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0493: Vulnerability in russh"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">No limit on the size of a decompressed SSH packet</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/russh/RUSTSEC-2026-0493.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/russh/RUSTSEC-2026-0493.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0493">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0493
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>No limit on the size of a decompressed SSH packet</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-05-29">
May 29, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">

</time>

<dt id="package">Package</dt>

</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>

</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>

<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>

</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/ssh.html">#ssh</a>
<a href="/keywords/zlib.html">#zlib</a>
<a href="/keywords/compression.html">#compression</a>
<a href="/keywords/decompression-bomb.html">#decompression-bomb</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-46702">CVE-2026-46702</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wwx6-x28x-8259">GHSA-wwx6-x28x-8259</a>
</li>

</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

<a href="https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/commit/2692a6f66edafaf63f463c412b45d8490f7ad06e">
https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/commit/2692a6f66edafaf63f463c412b45d8490f7ad06e
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>7.5 <span class="tag high">
HIGH
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>

<dd>High</dd>

<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.61.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.34.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>With <code>zlib</code> or <code>zlib@openssh.com</code> compression negotiated, the packet length that russh checks applies to the compressed payload only. The decompressor kept growing its output buffer until the whole payload was inflated, with no limit of its own, so a packet that is small on the wire could expand to hundreds of megabytes before it was parsed.</p>
<p>The default server configuration offers both zlib methods, and a client that lists compression first can select it during the initial key exchange. That client can then send such a packet after <code>NEWKEYS</code> but before authenticating, so a russh server with default settings can be made to use large amounts of memory by an unauthenticated peer. A malicious server can do the same to a client that negotiated compression.</p>
<p>From 0.61.1, decompressed output is capped at the same maximum as an uncompressed packet (256 KiB plus a few bytes of header room), and larger packets are rejected with <code>Error::PacketSize</code>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.60.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A server that answers a keyboard-interactive authentication request with prompts (<code>Auth::Partial</code>, as a TOTP or other second-factor login does) then parses the client's <code>SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_INFO_RESPONSE</code>. russh read the number of responses from that message and reserved a vector of that many entries before reading any of them. A client that has not authenticated can send a very large count in a packet that carries no responses at all, and the server tries to allocate several gigabytes and may be killed for running out of memory.</p>
<p>Servers whose handler keeps the default behaviour, which rejects keyboard-interactive authentication, are not affected. From 0.60.1 the count is limited by how many responses the remaining packet data could actually hold.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>
<entry>
<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0495: Vulnerability in russh</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0495.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0495.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0495: Vulnerability in russh"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Agent server signs with confirm-constrained keys without calling `Agent::confirm`</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/russh/RUSTSEC-2026-0495.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/russh/RUSTSEC-2026-0495.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0495">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0495
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Agent server signs with confirm-constrained keys without calling <code>Agent::confirm</code>
</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-10-30">
October 30, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/russh.html">russh</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/russh">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/ssh-agent.html">#ssh-agent</a>
<a href="/keywords/confirm.html">#confirm</a>
<a href="/keywords/constraint.html">#constraint</a>
<a href="/keywords/authorization.html">#authorization</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-116794">CVE-2026-116794</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-vp5q-ff62-jwqv">GHSA-vp5q-ff62-jwqv</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/commit/62db6e7defd6d244159da962ccccb029e785ce0f">
https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/commit/62db6e7defd6d244159da962ccccb029e785ce0f
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.65.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.50.0-beta.12</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>A key added to an SSH agent with the confirm constraint (what <code>ssh-add -c</code> sends) must not be used to sign until the user approves each use. russh's agent server (<code>russh::keys::agent::server</code>) accepted the constraint, but when a sign request arrived for such a key it used a fixed <code>true</code> in place of the result of <code>Agent::confirm</code>, so the application's confirmation step never ran. This started in 0.50.0-beta.12; 0.50.0-beta.8 to beta.11 still called <code>confirm</code>, and earlier russh releases have no agent server.</p>
<p>Any process that can reach the agent, including a remote host when the agent is forwarded, can then sign with the protected key without the user's approval, for example to log in elsewhere as the user. The private key itself is not disclosed. Agents whose <code>Agent::confirm</code> keeps the default, which always returns <code>true</code>, behave the same before and after the fix.</p>
<p>From 0.65.0 the agent server calls <code>Agent::confirm</code> for confirm-constrained keys and refuses to sign when it returns <code>false</code>. Implementations that left <code>confirm</code> unimplemented (for example as <code>todo!()</code>) will now reach that code.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0492: Vulnerability in russh</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0492.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0492.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0492: Vulnerability in russh"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Client `Handler` callbacks run for channels the client never opened</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/russh/RUSTSEC-2026-0492.md">History</a> ⋅

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0492
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Client <code>Handler</code> callbacks run for channels the client never opened</p>

</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>7.5 <span class="tag high">
HIGH
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>

<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>

<dt>Confidentiality Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>

<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:N">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:N</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.63.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>On the client side, russh passed channel messages from the server (data, extended data, EOF, close, success, failure, open failure and the exit-status, exit-signal and xon-xoff requests) to the application's <code>client::Handler</code> callbacks without checking that the channel number belonged to a channel the client had opened and the server had confirmed. The server side already performed this check.</p>
<p>A malicious or compromised SSH server can therefore send these messages for any channel number, including the next number the client will allocate (they are assigned in order) or one it has already closed. An application that tracks per-channel state can be made to record a false exit status, see a channel close before its real output arrives, or panic when it looks up a channel it does not know.</p>
<p>From 0.63.1 the client ignores channel messages for channels that are not established and confirmed, and handles an open failure only for a channel it is actually opening.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0510: Vulnerability in vodozemac</title>

<p>Panic when decoding malformed Ed25519 keys from Base64</p>

</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>

</li>
</ul>
</dd>

<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>vodozemac::Ed25519PublicKey::from_base64</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.11.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt>
<code>vodozemac::Ed25519SecretKey::from_base64</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.11.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>In version 0.11.1 and earlier of vodozemac, Ed25519PublicKey::from_base64() and
Ed25519SecretKey::from_base64() panic on malformed input instead of returning an
error, if the input decodes to 31 or 33 bytes instead of the expected 32 bytes.
An attacker who can supply a key to an application using vodozemac can crash it.</p>
<h2>Impact</h2>
<p>Ed25519 public keys are routinely received from other Matrix users and servers,
so a malicious party can publish a crafted key that crashes clients parsing it.</p>
<p>The impact is limited to denial of service; no key disclosure or signature
forgery is possible.</p>

</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.0-beta.1, &#60;=0.9.0-beta.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt>
<code>bsv_rs::transaction::Beef::verify_valid</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.8.5</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.0-beta.1, &#60;=0.9.0-beta.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt>
<code>bsv_rs::transaction::BeefTx::from_raw_tx</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.8.5</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.0-beta.1, &#60;=0.9.0-beta.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>
<code>Beef::merge_raw_tx</code> and <code>BeefTx::from_raw_tx</code> accept raw bytes without a
<code>Result</code>. For bytes that are not exactly one transaction (a transaction
followed by bytes, a cut transaction, bytes that are no transaction at all),
<code>Beef::sort_txs</code>, <code>verify_structure</code>, <code>verify_valid</code> and <code>is_valid</code> could
answer valid, even with no BUMP and no parent in the BEEF, and, where the entry
carried a BUMP index, treat the hash of those bytes as a proven transaction id;
an entry that names it as a parent was answered valid too.</p>
<p>The wire readers (<code>Beef::from_binary</code>, <code>verify_stream</code>,
<code>Transaction::from_beef</code>, <code>from_atomic_beef</code>) parse every entry and are not
affected. Reaching this needs an application that passes bytes it did not
parse to <code>merge_raw_tx</code> or <code>BeefTx::from_raw_tx</code> and then relies on the verdict
or on the transaction the BEEF hands back.</p>
<p>0.8.5 refuses such an entry at all four verdict doors, with or without a BUMP
index, and every entry that names it as a parent. Where upgrading is not
possible, do not rely on <code>is_valid</code> or <code>verify_valid</code> alone for such an entry:
check that the bytes are exactly one transaction before merging them.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0497: Vulnerability in bsv-rs</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0497.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0497.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0497: Vulnerability in bsv-rs"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Transaction::from_json holds an input txid that is not 64 hex digits and writes the input as spending the zero txid</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/bsv-rs/RUSTSEC-2026-0497.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0497
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Transaction::from_json holds an input txid that is not 64 hex digits and writes the input as spending the zero txid</p>
</span>

<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>

</li>

<code>&#62;=0.9.0-beta.1, &#60;=0.9.0-beta.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>
<code>Transaction::from_json</code>, for a document with no <code>hex</code> member, held each
input's <code>txid</code> member as given. An input <code>txid</code> that is not 64 hex digits
(<code>&quot;zz&quot;</code>, <code>&quot;&quot;</code>, 63 or 65 digits, characters that are not hex digits) answered
<code>Ok</code>: <code>source_txid</code> and <code>to_json</code> gave the text back, while <code>to_binary</code> wrote
the input as spending the txid of 32 zero bytes and <code>id()</code> hashed those bytes.
Documents naming different texts were one transaction, spending an outpoint
none of them named.</p>
<p>An application that reads a peer's transaction document with <code>from_json</code> and
relies both on the text of its inputs and on its bytes or <code>id()</code> is affected;
one that reads only documents it produced is not.</p>
<p>0.8.6 refuses such a txid (<code>Invalid txid of input N: expected 64 hex digits</code>).
Where upgrading is not possible, require each input's <code>txid</code> to be 64 ASCII hex
digits before calling <code>from_json</code>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0498: Vulnerability in bsv-rs</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0498.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="crypto-failure"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0498.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0498: Vulnerability in bsv-rs"/>

<summary xml:lang="en">Transaction readers accept trailing bytes, so a parsed transaction can carry another transaction id</summary>

</ul>

</dt>

</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>
<code>Transaction::from_binary</code> and <code>Transaction::from_hex</code> read one transaction
from the front of their input, did not check that the input ended there, and
kept the whole input as the transaction's cached serialization: <code>to_binary</code>,
<code>to_hex</code> and <code>id()</code> then answered for bytes that are not the transaction's.
<code>Transaction::from_json</code>, given a <code>hex</code> field, reads it through <code>from_hex</code> and
answers the same. <code>from_ef</code>, <code>from_hex_ef</code> and <code>parse_script_offsets</code> also
read a prefix.</p>
<p>An application that takes bytes or hex from outside, parses them with these
readers and then uses <code>id()</code> or the serialization could be given one
transaction under another identity: the same parsed fields, a different id.
An application that passes them only bytes it produced or has already checked
is not affected.</p>
<p>0.8.5 refuses a byte after the transaction; <code>Transaction::from_reader</code> reads
from the front of a longer buffer. Where upgrading is not possible, parse with
<code>Transaction::from_binary</code>, call <code>tx.invalidate_caches()</code> (public from 0.3.16)
and require <code>tx.to_binary() == bytes</code>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

</ul>

<dt>
<code>bsv_rs::primitives::ec::Signature::from_der</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>

</li>

<code>bsv_rs::wallet::ProtoWallet::verify_signature</code>
</dt>

<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.0-beta.1, &#60;=0.9.0-beta.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>
<code>Signature::from_der</code> read a DER-encoded ECDSA signature leniently. It read as
the signature: the signature followed by other bytes; a sequence length
shorter than its contents, or longer with bytes after them (a length byte with
its high bit set among them); and an R or S preceded by a zero byte it did not
need. It read a lone-zero R or S as zero.</p>
<p>
<code>messages::verify</code>, <code>compat::bsm::verify_message_der</code>,
<code>auth::Certificate::verify</code>, <code>auth::VerifiableCertificate::verify</code>,
<code>wallet::ProtoWallet::verify_signature</code> and
<code>kvstore::KVStoreInterpreter::verify_signature</code> read through it, and each
verified such a form of a valid signature. Given one signature a key holder
made, anyone could produce other byte strings that verify as that signature
over the same data. No form verifies over other data: an application that acts
on the verdict alone is not affected; one that compares, hashes or stores a
signature's bytes is.</p>
<p>0.8.6 refuses each form as <code>Error::InvalidSignature</code>. A signature's bytes are
still not unique after the patch: the same signature with S replaced by n - S
verifies, so an application that keys on the bytes also requires
<code>Signature::is_low_s()</code>.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0501: Vulnerability in bsv-rs</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0501.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="crypto-failure"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0501.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0501: Vulnerability in bsv-rs"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Shamir backup recovery accepts a share at x = 0, so a supplied share can substitute the recovered private key</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/bsv-rs/RUSTSEC-2026-0501.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0501
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Shamir backup recovery accepts a share at x = 0, so a supplied share can substitute the recovered private key</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>

<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>

</time>

</li>

</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>9.1 <span class="tag critical">
CRITICAL
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>

<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>

</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.8.7, &#60;0.9.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;0.9.0-beta.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>Affected Functions</dt>
<dd>Version</dd>
<dt>
<code>bsv_rs::primitives::bsv::shamir::KeyShares::from_backup_format</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.2.0, &#60;0.8.7</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.0-beta.1, &#60;=0.9.0-beta.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt>
<code>bsv_rs::primitives::bsv::shamir::KeyShares::recover_private_key</code>
</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.2.0, &#60;0.8.7</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.9.0-beta.1, &#60;=0.9.0-beta.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>
<code>KeyShares::from_backup_format</code> (the Shamir backup share reader, public since
0.2.0, the crate's first release) accepted a share whose x coordinate is 0,
and <code>KeyShares::recover_private_key</code> interpolates the shares at x = 0 to
recover the key. At an x of 0 the interpolation answers that share's y value
alone, so an attacker who supplies one share in that form — with the share's
integrity tag ground to match the honest set's — chooses the recovered key
outright. The non-canonical zero x = p works the same way, and a repeated x
past the threshold window also passed.</p>
<p>An application that recovers a Shamir backup from shares it did not produce —
a custodian's medium, a share pasted by hand, a set restored from another
writer — could be handed the attacker's key as the recovered one. A set the
crate's own writer produces never carries such a share; the door is the
reader.</p>
<p>0.8.7 refuses such a set before interpolating (the 0.9 line's next prerelease
carries the same refusal). Where upgrading is not possible, before recovering
split each share on <code>.</code> into four fields, base58-decode the x field, and
refuse the set if it decodes to zero (the one-character field <code>1</code>, or a value
at or above the field prime) or if two shares carry the same x.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0502: Vulnerability in sqlcipher-src</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0502.html</id>
