</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=420.0.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;419.0.3</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>SQLite versions before 3.54.0 can return uninitialized page-cache memory when a process opens a database file together with a sibling write-ahead-log (<code>-wal</code>) file whose page size differs from the database's. A crafted pair of files can make ordinary queries return stale bytes from the process's memory. The attack vector is local and needs the victim to open the files.</p>
<p>
<code>sqlcipher-src</code> ships the SQLCipher amalgamation (SQLCipher is built on SQLite and contains the same <code>sqlite3WalReadFrame</code> code) for <code>-sys</code> crates to compile, so a build that uses it is susceptible to the vulnerability. The releases <code>419.0.3</code> to <code>419.0.6</code> bundle SQLCipher <code>4.19.0</code> on SQLite <code>3.53.4</code> and are affected. <code>sqlcipher-src</code> was updated in <code>420.0.0</code> (SQLCipher <code>4.20.0</code> on SQLite <code>3.54.1</code>), which contains the fix. The releases <code>0.1.0</code> and <code>0.1.1</code> contain no SQLCipher source. <code>libsqlite3-sys</code>
<code>0.39.0</code> and <code>0.39.1</code> depend on <code>sqlcipher-src =419.0.6</code> for their <code>bundled-sqlcipher</code> feature.</p>
<p>Only a process that opens a database file, with its <code>-wal</code>, that a less trusted party could have written is exposed.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0504: Vulnerability in hickory-proto</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0504.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>

<summary xml:lang="en">Inverted NSEC3 comparison in DNSSEC validation (hickory-proto 0.25)</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0504
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Inverted NSEC3 comparison in DNSSEC validation (hickory-proto 0.25)</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-proto.html">hickory-proto</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-proto">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec3.html">#nsec3</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123601">CVE-2026-123601</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-588m-chg6-8jqj">GHSA-588m-chg6-8jqj</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/87ee4f81aa9689d81847770d3a74e13982c9bcdc">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/87ee4f81aa9689d81847770d3a74e13982c9bcdc
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
no patched versions
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.25.0</code>
</li>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>In hickory-proto 0.25.0 through 0.26.0-alpha.1, DNSSEC validation handled the last NSEC3 record of a zone's hash chain with its two comparisons reversed. That record was treated as covering almost every hash it does not cover, so an attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses could replay it, with its RRSIG, to forge proofs that existing names have no records.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in hickory-proto 0.25 (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor 0.25) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0505: Vulnerability in hickory-proto</title>

<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Up to 0.26.0-alpha.1, hickory-proto's DNSSEC validation matched NSEC3 records to a name only by the hashed first label of the NSEC3 owner name, without checking that the rest of the owner name was the zone in question. A signed NSEC3 record from any zone the attacker controls could therefore be used, by someone who can answer or modify DNS responses, to prove that a name in another zone does not exist.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in older hickory-proto releases (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Up to 0.26.0-alpha.1, hickory-proto's NSEC validation accepted an NXDOMAIN response as secure even when the covering NSEC record's next domain name was below the queried name, which proves the name exists as an empty non-terminal. Because the response code is not signed, someone who can modify DNS responses could turn a genuine signed NODATA response into an accepted NXDOMAIN.</p>
<p>From 0.26.0-beta.1 the DNSSEC validation code lives in the hickory-net crate, where this was fixed in hickory-net 0.26.2. No hickory-proto release contains a fix. Users of DNSSEC validation in older hickory-proto releases (directly or through hickory-resolver or hickory-recursor) should move to the 0.26.2 or later releases, which validate through hickory-net.</p>

license.

<summary xml:lang="en">`Resolver::lookup` and `lookup_ip` returned records that failed DNSSEC validation</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">

<code>Resolver::lookup</code> and <code>lookup_ip</code> returned records that failed DNSSEC validation</p>

<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>

<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>With DNSSEC validation enabled, <code>Resolver::lookup()</code> and <code>Resolver::lookup_ip()</code> returned <code>Ok</code> with the records even when validation found the response bogus. The result was only visible in each record's proof status, which callers had to inspect themselves.</p>
<p>Applications that enable validation and treat a successful lookup as validated data would therefore accept forged or tampered answers from anyone who can answer or modify their DNS traffic.</p>
<p>From 0.26.2 a lookup whose records are bogus fails with a &quot;DNSSEC validation failed&quot; error (<code>DnsError::DnssecBogus</code>), the error is not retried, and the hickory-server forwarder answers SERVFAIL for it.</p>

license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0513: Vulnerability in hickory-resolver</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0513.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0513.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0513: Vulnerability in hickory-resolver"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Unbounded retries on truncated responses in the name server pool</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-resolver/RUSTSEC-2026-0513.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/hickory-resolver/RUSTSEC-2026-0513.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0513">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0513
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Unbounded retries on truncated responses in the name server pool</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-resolver.html">hickory-resolver</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-resolver">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/truncation.html">#truncation</a>
<a href="/keywords/retry.html">#retry</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123604">CVE-2026-123604</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-6w6g-hm98-mhgm">GHSA-6w6g-hm98-mhgm</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/f9a7bc5d188e5b680b09aea91009d2fbf3fec58a">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/f9a7bc5d188e5b680b09aea91009d2fbf3fec58a
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When an upstream answer had the TC (truncated) bit set, the resolver's name server pool disabled UDP and sent the query again. It did this for every truncated answer, including answers that already came over TCP, and it kept no count of retries.</p>
<p>A name server that sets TC on every answer, which anyone running an authoritative server the resolver queries can do, kept each lookup re-sending queries over the same connection until the request deadline (5 seconds by default) ran out. This uses up resolver resources and sends many queries per lookup.</p>
<p>From 0.26.2 a truncated answer received after UDP has already been disabled ends the lookup with <code>NetError::Truncated</code> instead of retrying again.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/74062f3a3ef6904b64717dd025945702b2cf4133">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/74062f3a3ef6904b64717dd025945702b2cf4133
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/47cd005b8f3d190134bbb943d2328ef8a83ade8c">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/47cd005b8f3d190134bbb943d2328ef8a83ade8c
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When a referral listed name servers outside the delegated zone and gave no glue addresses, the recursor looked up A and AAAA records for every listed name server, recursing into each one. Only the chain-depth limits applied. There was no cap on how many name servers it chased per referral or on the total upstream work per client query.</p>
<p>Someone who controls a zone can return referrals that list many glueless name servers in a victim's domain, and nest them, so that each client query makes the recursor send a large number of queries to the victim's servers (the NXNSAttack pattern). The same can exhaust the recursor's own resources.</p>
<p>This affects the recursive resolver, which is in hickory-resolver behind the opt-in <code>recursor</code> feature from 0.26.0-beta.1. Earlier releases shipped it as the separate hickory-recursor crate.</p>
<p>From 0.26.2 the recursor follows at most 5 glueless name servers per delegation and sends at most 200 upstream queries per client request.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0516: Vulnerability in hickory-resolver</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0516.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0516.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0516: Vulnerability in hickory-resolver"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Recursor kept querying cyclic glueless sibling delegations</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-resolver/RUSTSEC-2026-0516.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0516
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Recursor kept querying cyclic glueless sibling delegations</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-resolver.html">hickory-resolver</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-resolver">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/recursor.html">#recursor</a>
<a href="/keywords/amplification.html">#amplification</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123586">CVE-2026-123586</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-cx5j-p54p-q756">GHSA-cx5j-p54p-q756</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/308dcfd5cd9bfaa50c956427380160505e5de532">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/308dcfd5cd9bfaa50c956427380160505e5de532
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.26.0-beta.1</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Two zones can each delegate to name servers named only inside the other zone, with no glue records (the cyclic sibling case in RFC 9471 section 2.3). Such delegations can never be resolved, but the recursor did not stop when it came back to a zone it was already resolving. It kept sending queries until the per-request limits ran out, and zero TTLs on the referral NS records made this worse.</p>
<p>Someone who controls two such zones can make a recursor send many upstream queries for each client query.</p>
<p>This affects the recursive resolver, which is in hickory-resolver behind the opt-in <code>recursor</code> feature from 0.26.0-beta.1. Earlier releases shipped it as the separate hickory-recursor crate.</p>
<p>From 0.26.2 each client request tracks the zones on its resolution stack and refuses to enter a zone that is already on it.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0519: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0519.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0519.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0519: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">NXDOMAIN accepted for an empty non-terminal that the NSEC record proves exists</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0519.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0519.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0519">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0519
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>NXDOMAIN accepted for an empty non-terminal that the NSEC record proves exists</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec.html">#nsec</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123594">CVE-2026-123594</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-86vr-jm6c-7cpg">GHSA-86vr-jm6c-7cpg</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/bfbe7ede4021965526f065be9b415945ce575e29">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/bfbe7ede4021965526f065be9b415945ce575e29
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When an NSEC record's next domain name is below the queried name, the queried name exists as an empty non-terminal (RFC 8020 section 3.1). The NSEC validator did not check for this case and accepted an NXDOMAIN response backed by such a record as secure.</p>
<p>The response code is not covered by any signature. An attacker who can modify DNS responses can take a genuine signed NODATA response for an empty non-terminal, change its response code to NXDOMAIN, and have the validator accept that an existing name does not exist.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix rejects NXDOMAIN as bogus, and validates NODATA as secure, when the covering NSEC record's next domain name is below the queried name and is not the zone apex. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0508: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0508.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0508.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0508: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">NSEC wraparound detection trusted an unvalidated SOA record</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0508.md">History</a> ⋅

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0508
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>NSEC wraparound detection trusted an unvalidated SOA record</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec.html">#nsec</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123587">CVE-2026-123587</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-qw83-5pm2-ggp5">GHSA-qw83-5pm2-ggp5</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/e5ca40a7ef149ba5a79bc6f4916f114d153ce9ab">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/e5ca40a7ef149ba5a79bc6f4916f114d153ce9ab
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.26.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>The last NSEC record in a zone points back to the zone apex. To recognise it, DNSSEC validation treated an NSEC record as covering every name after its owner when its next domain name equalled the owner name of an SOA record in the response. That SOA record did not have to pass validation.</p>
<p>An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can replay a genuine NSEC record and its RRSIG, add a forged SOA record whose name equals that NSEC record's next domain name, and so make the validator accept that any name sorting after the NSEC owner does not exist.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix recognises a wraparound NSEC record from the chain itself (its next domain name sorts at or before its owner and is the zone of the queried name) and only uses validated SOA records. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0518: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0518.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0518.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0518: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">DNSSEC validation cache could reuse an Insecure result for a different name</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0518.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0518.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0518">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0518
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>DNSSEC validation cache could reuse an Insecure result for a different name</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/cache.html">#cache</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123592">CVE-2026-123592</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-rx82-4p2j-j5cv">GHSA-rx82-4p2j-j5cv</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/96b87c9ab0f10c1331cef56f88742eb00bce71e1">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/96b87c9ab0f10c1331cef56f88742eb00bce71e1
</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/9c82ce0c529d33ef4b3d6507e3b8dbbb5cd0dd48">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/9c82ce0c529d33ef4b3d6507e3b8dbbb5cd0dd48
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>The DNSSEC validation cache used a 64-bit hash of the query as its key, and two keys with the same hash counted as equal. The <code>Hash</code> implementation of <code>Name</code> hashed the bytes of the labels but not where one label ends and the next begins, so names that differ only in the placement of dots (such as <code>ab.c.</code> and <code>a.bc.</code>) hashed to the same value.</p>
<p>An attacker who controls an unsigned delegation whose name collides in this way with a name in a signed zone can first get an Insecure result cached for their own name. A forged response for the signed name is then served from that cache entry without signature checks and returned with the AD bit clear, where validation should have failed.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix stores the query in the cache key and compares it on lookup. The <code>Hash</code> implementations of <code>Label</code>, <code>Name</code> and <code>LowerName</code> in hickory-proto were also changed to include label boundaries. Both are in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>
<entry>
<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0509: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>

<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/d2ee774a789193010f109d3d463adf72c4c261ab">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/d2ee774a789193010f109d3d463adf72c4c261ab
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>7.5 <span class="tag high">
HIGH
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Scope</dt>
<dd>Unchanged</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:N">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:N</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When DNSSEC validation used NSEC3 records to prove that a name or record type does not exist, it compared only the hashed first label of each NSEC3 owner name with the hash of the name being checked. The rest of the owner name, which says which zone the record belongs to, was not checked.</p>
<p>Someone who controls any DNSSEC-signed zone can publish a signed NSEC3 record whose first label is the hash of a name in another zone. An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can then use it to make the validator accept that the name in the other zone does not exist.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix requires a matching NSEC3 record to belong to a zone that contains the checked name, and ignores NSEC3 records whose owner is not directly below the RRSIG signer name. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0506: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0506.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0506.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0506: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">Inverted NSEC3 comparison lets one record &quot;prove&quot; that almost any name does not exist</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0506.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0506.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0506">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0506
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Inverted NSEC3 comparison lets one record &quot;prove&quot; that almost any name does not exist</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/nsec3.html">#nsec3</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123601">CVE-2026-123601</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-588m-chg6-8jqj">GHSA-588m-chg6-8jqj</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/87ee4f81aa9689d81847770d3a74e13982c9bcdc">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/87ee4f81aa9689d81847770d3a74e13982c9bcdc
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When DNSSEC validation looks for the NSEC3 record that covers a hashed name, the last record in the zone's hash chain needs special handling, because its next hashed owner name sorts before its own hash. For that record the two comparisons were reversed: it was treated as covering every hash below its owner hash or above its next hash, which is mostly the part of the chain it does not cover.</p>
<p>An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can replay that one signed NSEC3 record, with its RRSIG, from the target zone and use it in forged negative answers. The validator then accepts proof that names which do exist have no records.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> (resolver, recursor and client) when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>. The same code was in hickory-proto before it moved to hickory-net.</p>
<p>The fix corrects the comparison in <code>find_covering_record</code>. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
</content>
</entry>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0507: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0507.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0507.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0507: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">RRSIG signer name was not checked against the zone of the signed records</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/hickory-net/RUSTSEC-2026-0507.md">History</a> ⋅

<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0507">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0507
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>RRSIG signer name was not checked against the zone of the signed records</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-net.html">hickory-net</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-net">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/dnssec.html">#dnssec</a>
<a href="/keywords/rrsig.html">#rrsig</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123605">CVE-2026-123605</a>
</li>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-2vgh-3wfw-qj7c">GHSA-2vgh-3wfw-qj7c</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/019535e011131c52d19cb6a2c7a2d31270271135">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/019535e011131c52d19cb6a2c7a2d31270271135
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>DNSSEC validation checked an RRset against the DNSKEYs of whatever zone was named in the RRSIG's Signer's Name field. RFC 4035 section 5.3.1 requires that name to be the zone that contains the RRset, but this was not checked.</p>
<p>Anyone who controls a DNSSEC-signed zone, and can answer or modify DNS responses, can therefore sign records for names in an unrelated zone with their own keys and have the validator accept them as secure.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> (resolver, recursor and client) when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix ignores an RRSIG whose signer name is not the RRset's owner name or one of its ancestors. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

</content>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0520: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0520.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>

<summary xml:lang="en">DNSSEC validation accepted a validly signed but unrelated RRset as the answer</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">

RUSTSEC-2026-0520
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>DNSSEC validation accepted a validly signed but unrelated RRset as the answer</p>
</span>

<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>

</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>5.3 <span class="tag medium">
MEDIUM
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>

<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>

<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.0#CVSS:3.0/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:L/A:N">CVSS:3.0/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:L/A:N</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When a response had a non-empty answer section, DNSSEC validation treated it as a positive answer as long as the records in it validated. It did not check that any of those records had the queried name and either the queried type or type CNAME.</p>
<p>An attacker who can answer or modify DNS responses can therefore return a genuine, signed RRset for some other name or type, and have it accepted as a validated answer to the query instead of being handled as a response with no matching data.</p>
<p>This affects DNSSEC validation through <code>DnssecDnsHandle</code> when built with <code>dnssec-ring</code> or <code>dnssec-aws-lc-rs</code>.</p>
<p>The fix requires at least one answer record that matches the query name and is either of the queried type or a CNAME, both for positive responses and when NSEC records are checked. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0517: Vulnerability in hickory-net</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0517.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="denial-of-service"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0517.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0517: Vulnerability in hickory-net"/>

<summary xml:lang="en">Spoofed malformed UDP datagrams abort DNS lookups</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0517
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Spoofed malformed UDP datagrams abort DNS lookups</p>

</a>
</li>

<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>5.9 <span class="tag medium">
MEDIUM
</span>

<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>

<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H</a>

<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.26.2</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.26.0</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>While waiting for a reply on a UDP connection, the client parsed every datagram that arrived from the name server's address and port before checking its message ID. If parsing failed, the attempt ended with an error instead of dropping the datagram and waiting for the real reply. The receive loop also gave up after three datagrams.</p>
<p>An off-path attacker who can guess the client's source port, but not the transaction ID, can send one malformed datagram with a spoofed source address and make the pending lookup fail.</p>
<p>The fix reads the message ID from the first two bytes before parsing, ignores short datagrams and datagrams with the wrong ID, keeps listening until the outer timeout, and only then parses the response. It is in 0.26.2.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0521
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Recursor followed every glueless name server in a referral (no fixed hickory-recursor release)</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>

<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
no patched versions
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.25.0-alpha.3</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>For referrals whose name servers are outside the delegated zone and come without glue, the recursor resolved every listed name server, with no cap on referral width and no per-query work budget. Someone who controls a zone can use this to make each client query send a large number of queries to a victim's servers (the NXNSAttack pattern), or to exhaust the recursor.</p>
<p>The recursor code moved into hickory-resolver (opt-in <code>recursor</code> feature) in 0.26.0-beta.1, and the fix is in hickory-resolver 0.26.2. No hickory-recursor release contains a fix, so users should move to hickory-resolver 0.26.2 or later with the <code>recursor</code> feature.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0522: Vulnerability in hickory-recursor</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0522.html</id>

<summary xml:lang="en">Recursor kept querying cyclic glueless sibling delegations (no fixed hickory-recursor release)</summary>

<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0522
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>Recursor kept querying cyclic glueless sibling delegations (no fixed hickory-recursor release)</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-09-03">
September 3, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/hickory-recursor.html">hickory-recursor</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/hickory-recursor">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/denial-of-service.html">denial-of-service</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/dns.html">#dns</a>
<a href="/keywords/recursor.html">#recursor</a>
<a href="/keywords/amplification.html">#amplification</a>
</dd>
<dt id="aliases">Aliases</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-123586">CVE-2026-123586</a>
</li>
<li>

</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/308dcfd5cd9bfaa50c956427380160505e5de532">
https://github.com/hickory-dns/hickory-dns/commit/308dcfd5cd9bfaa50c956427380160505e5de532
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
no patched versions
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.25.0-alpha.3</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>Two zones can each delegate to name servers named only inside the other zone, with no glue records (RFC 9471 section 2.3). The recursor did not stop when it came back to a zone it was already resolving, so someone who controls two such zones can make it send many upstream queries for each client query.</p>
<p>The recursor code moved into hickory-resolver (opt-in <code>recursor</code> feature) in 0.26.0-beta.1, and the fix is in hickory-resolver 0.26.2. No hickory-recursor release contains a fix, so users should move to hickory-resolver 0.26.2 or later with the <code>recursor</code> feature.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>

<entry>
<title>RUSTSEC-2026-0503: Vulnerability in eazip</title>
<id>https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0503.html</id>
<updated>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</updated>
<category term="privilege-escalation"/>
<link href="https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0503.html" rel="alternate" type="text/html" title="RUSTSEC-2026-0503: Vulnerability in eazip"/>
<published>2026-11-12T12:00:00+00:00</published>
<summary xml:lang="en">eazip: symlink-target validation checks the wrong argument, allowing extraction path escapes on Windows</summary>
<content xml:lang="en" type="html">
<article>
<span class="floating-menu">
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/commits/main/crates/eazip/RUSTSEC-2026-0503.md">History</a> ⋅
<a href="https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db/edit/main/crates/eazip/RUSTSEC-2026-0503.md">Edit</a> ⋅
<a href="https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/RUSTSEC-2026-0503">JSON (OSV)</a>
</span>
<header>
<h1>
RUSTSEC-2026-0503
</h1>
<span class="subtitle">
<p>eazip: symlink-target validation checks the wrong argument, allowing extraction path escapes on Windows</p>
</span>
</header>
<dl>
<dt id="reported">Reported</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-07">
November 7, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="issued">Issued</dt>
<dd>
<time datetime="2026-11-12">
November 12, 2026
</time>
</dd>
<dt id="package">Package</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/packages/eazip.html">eazip</a>
(<a href="https://crates.io/crates/eazip">crates.io</a>)
</dd>
<dt id="type">Type</dt>
<dd>
Vulnerability
</dd>
<dt id="categories">Categories</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="/categories/privilege-escalation.html">privilege-escalation</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="keywords">Keywords</dt>
<dd>
<a href="/keywords/symlink.html">#symlink</a>
<a href="/keywords/zip-slip.html">#zip-slip</a>
<a href="/keywords/windows.html">#windows</a>
<a href="/keywords/path-escape.html">#path-escape</a>
<a href="/keywords/extraction.html">#extraction</a>
</dd>
<dt id="details">References</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<a href="https://github.com/a1phyr/eazip/releases/tag/v0.2.7">
https://github.com/a1phyr/eazip/releases/tag/v0.2.7
</a>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_score">CVSS Score</dt>
<dd>8.6 <span class="tag high">
HIGH
</span>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss_details">CVSS Details</dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>Attack Vector</dt>
<dd>Network</dd>
<dt>Attack Complexity</dt>
<dd>Low</dd>
<dt>Privileges Required</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>User Interaction</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Scope</dt>
<dd>Changed</dd>
<dt>Confidentiality Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
<dt>Integrity Impact</dt>
<dd>High</dd>
<dt>Availability Impact</dt>
<dd>None</dd>
</dl>
</dd>
<dt id="cvss">CVSS Vector</dt>
<dd>
<a href="https://www.first.org/cvss/calculator/3.1#CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:N/I:H/A:N">CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:N/I:H/A:N</a>
</dd>
<dt id="patched">Patched</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#62;=0.2.7</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
<dt id="unaffected">Unaffected</dt>
<dd>
<ul>
<li>
<code>&#60;0.2.5</code>
</li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
<h3 id="description">Description</h3>
<p>When extracting archives, <code>eazip</code> validates symlink entries before writing
them. The validation function <code>validate_symlink()</code> (<code>src/utils/mod.rs</code>) scans
for backslash and NUL bytes, but passes the symlink's <em>name</em> where the
symlink's <em>target</em> was intended:</p>
<pre>
<code class="language-rust">memchr2(b'\\', b'\0', name.as_bytes()) // should be target.as_bytes()
</code>
</pre>
<p>As a result, symlink <em>targets</em> containing backslash or NUL bytes pass
validation. On Windows, backslash is a path separator, so targets such as
<code>..\..\..\Windows\system32\evil.exe</code> also defeat the remaining depth check,
which splits on <code>/</code> only. A crafted archive can therefore create symlinks
that resolve outside the extraction directory — including UNC targets such as
<code>\\host\share\...</code> — redirecting writes to arbitrary filesystem locations
chosen by the attacker.</p>
<p>Applications that extract untrusted archives with <code>eazip</code> on Windows targets
are affected; on Unix targets backslash is not a separator, which limits
exposure there.</p>
<p>The vulnerability was introduced in version 0.2.5 and fixed in 0.2.7
(<a href="https://github.com/a1phyr/eazip/commit/0e4caa13d59f5ba15aaac332f5534260c3b8fc43">a1phyr/eazip@0e4caa13</a>),
which rejects backslash and NUL bytes in the symlink target. It was
privately reported to the maintainer on 2026-11-06 and credited in the
0.2.7 release notes.</p>
<p id="license" class="license">Advisory available under <a href="https://spdx.org/licenses/CC0-1.0.html">CC0-1.0</a>
license.
</p>
</article>
